**[00:49:19]** So, textual criticism.

**[00:49:21]** Let's talk a bit about textual criticism

**[00:49:23]** and why it's important.

**[00:49:24]** I have a whole section planned after this

**[00:49:25]** that we're going to be doing a very, very deep dive.

**[00:49:28]** But I wanted you to realize the truth about Erasmus,

**[00:49:30]** that he wasn't just some...

**[00:49:32]** Because it's easy to come away with this thing

**[00:49:34]** like Erasmus just was this noble hero,

**[00:49:36]** for pioneering Bible translations

**[00:49:39]** and the Protestant Reformation.

**[00:49:40]** Well, no, Erasmus was a Catholic priest, folks.

**[00:49:42]** He was not interested in supporting

**[00:49:44]** the Protestant Reformation and refuting the Catholic Church.

**[00:49:47]** He was hired by the Pope.

**[00:49:49]** Sure, he wanted some reform in the Catholic Church,

**[00:49:51]** but he was not going to stick his nose above the crowd

**[00:49:54]** and get his head chopped off.

**[00:49:56]** So, very, very important, because you still had

**[00:49:59]** Inquisition around that time.

**[00:50:01]** Either way, the point is, Erasmus was a Catholic priest,

**[00:50:04]** and God used him.

**[00:50:06]** Again, like he uses all things,

**[00:50:08]** he uses the good and the bad.

**[00:50:11]** He uses the foolish things to shame the wise.

**[00:50:13]** He used the Catholic priest who rushed to print,

**[00:50:15]** being hired by the Pope,

**[00:50:17]** with lots of errors,

**[00:50:19]** to create the next stage of the Bible,

**[00:50:22]** which, of course, eventually leads to the point

**[00:50:24]** where we all have Bibles in our pockets.

**[00:50:26]** All of these things have developed over time,

**[00:50:28]** and it's truly profound to see the history

**[00:50:30]** of how the Bible has been...

**[00:50:32]** I should say, Bible translations.

**[00:50:34]** Because, again, the Bible is perfect,

**[00:50:36]** and it's interesting to see the history of these translations

**[00:50:38]** as how they come into being,

**[00:50:40]** and how God has steered this process

**[00:50:42]** through things that you would not expect,

**[00:50:44]** which is the profound proof that, yes,

**[00:50:46]** God does preserve His Word.

**[00:50:48]** But He doesn't preserve His Word in a particular translation.

**[00:50:50]** That's the thing you've got to get through today.

**[00:50:53]** So, textual criticism, what is it?

**[00:50:55]** Textual criticism is a process of discerning the truth

**[00:50:57]** in a particular translation, creating a translation,

**[00:51:00]** by using multiple sources,

**[00:51:02]** and also a rule, or a set of rules,

**[00:51:05]** that basically help you determine

**[00:51:08]** what to prefer, what is true and what is not true.

**[00:51:11]** That's the important thing.

**[00:51:13]** Because, again, if you have 5,000 manuscripts

**[00:51:15]** from different times in history,

**[00:51:17]** what is your basis in determining

**[00:51:19]** which ones are to be preferred over the others?
